Q1 PACKAGE MAP (incremental finding)
@oai/cua 0.2.5 exports legacy . plus ./tinyskyAlt (the actual model facade) and ./tinyskyBrowser; dependencies:{} does NOT mean dependency-free: dependencies are rolled into dist. @oai/cua-repl 0.1.0 is a thin spawn/configuration wrapper, not the node_repl engine. Q2 FIRST-CALL API DOCUMENT
The initial “## Computer Use” document is STATIC Markdown, not generated from apiManifest. R/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:1-214 contains the TypeScript declarations (13-152), native platform notes (158-164), workflow (166-191), output (193-198), notes (200-214). Alternative core-node-repl.md is selected by nodeRepl.env.TINYSKY_ALT_INITIALIZE_DOCS; default is core-cua-repl. R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_cua/src/tinysky_alt/create_documentation.js:3-50 reads that file, then only for core-cua-repl appends confirmations: requestMeta['openai/confirmation_policies'].computer_use overrides the default if a nonempty string <=12000 UTF-8 bytes; training/orbit with no override suppress policy, otherwise docs/tinysky-alt-confirmations.md:1-78 is read. read_documentation.js:4-16 finds the package docs directory. Thus the header/TS/workflow are not pruned for a computer-only connection.
Bootstrap imports ./tinyskyAlt; globals.js:3-27 checks enabled surfaces, creates the API, aliases initialize=getState, and sets globalThis.cua. create_tinysky_alt.js:16-27 initializes browser/computer in parallel, then emits docs immediately. create_bootstrap_emitter.js:10-85 serializes writes, uses nodeRepl.write(text,'cua.core'|'cua.browser.<browserId>'|'cua.state'), emits core once, and caches browser.documentation() per browserId; emit:false suppresses state only, not first-use docs. rewriteDocumentation re-emits cached core and every already-seen browser document unless requestMeta object identity matches its prior write (87-101).
Selected-browser docs ARE service-generated. browser-client.js:9929-9936 sends get_browser_documentation; setupBrowserRuntime at 10215-10271 gets apiManifest and disabledMemberIds, constructs a proxy masking unsupported members, and calls trusted browser RPC. The actual vendor service used by cua is @oai/browser-desktop; its equivalent generator is scripts/browser-service.mjs:65373 onward (readBrowser:65387). The bundled copy inside @oai/cua provides a clear corresponding implementation at R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/scripts/browser-service.mjs:58024-58221: disabled members union browser-type unsupported defaults with apiSupportOverrides; undocumented members are hidden only in docs; documentManifest selects included/lookup guidance by browser type, capabilities and required API members; excludedDocumentation omits included text. API generator traverses reachable types from manifest.root, prints declarations and type text, omits undocumented/disabled members (58063-58120). readBrowser assembles Selected Browser identity/reuse advice, included docs, lookup index, capability index, generated API Reference and service addendum (58136-58190). setup_browser.js:9-18 excludes tab-claiming-chrome, tab-mentions-iab and (for cua-repl) confirmations, and hides Tab.ax docs, because facade/core handle those.
Own host rules are a separate MCP layer, NOT merged into the vendor Markdown or manifest: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/mcp/surface.mjs:209-261 prepends jsRulesFor to js tool.description, and publishes service-specific Chrome rules via profiles_list's description; withHostNotes appends host notes to server instructions (238-240). Profile entries expose key/readiness/extensionInstanceId, never Chrome directory (234-236). Rules differ between cua and vendor browser services (223-225; 91-110). Preserve this distinction when replacing packages; replacing docs alone must not remove host rules.

Q3 TRUSTED-SERVICE DELEGATION / nodeRepl
Sky facade: R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/project/cua/sky_js/src/sky.js:6-18 calls nodeRepl.rpc('sky',{type:'setup'}) and receives target/methods; 55-66 synthesizes methods sending {type:'execute',method,args}. Linux drag handles use {type:'drag_start'|'drag_move'|'drag_end',handle_id,point?} (67-89). Return conversions include base64/data-url screenshot and audio bytes and Linux tree reconstruction (94-127). In node_repl it requires RPC; standalone use outside nodeRepl can construct a local computer client (44-54).
Browser: R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/scripts/browser-client.js:10215-10311 sends rpc('browser',{method:'setup',params:{environment,undocumentedApiMembers,excludedDocumentation}}), then method execute or executeWithRecovery with flat commands. FunctionAgentTransport at 10013-10046 serializes command.toJSON(), adds positive client_timeout_ms, displays and strips side_effects, and rejects null/undefined responses with 'transport send returned empty response'. No function object goes over RPC: evaluate functions are source strings (6717-6751); binary images are serialized/base64 on service paths and reconstructed by client adapters. apiManifest determines which interfaces may be wrapped; a manifest interface with no runtime constructor throws (10068-10079); member disabling covers get/has/ownKeys/descriptors and recursively wraps returned API objects (10081-10212).
Actual vendor service: R/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:77562-77614 exports handleRpc(request), permits setup/execute/executeWithRecovery, setup returns credentialRecoveryErrorVersion:1, and ONLY BrowserCredentialRecoveryError becomes {ok:false,error:details}; other exceptions reject. Own service intentionally returns no recovery version and rejects ordinary failures, per /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-13-own-browser-service-design.md:61-65,275-312,369. This is not a place to put generic errors into {ok:false}; the vendor client accepts only the recovery-details schema (browser-client.js:189-242,10272-10284).
Boundary verified from EMBEDDED JS in installed Mach-O, without execution. Citation convention for N=/Users/new/Library/Application Support/cua/runtimes/26.928.40906-darwin-arm64/cua_node/bin/node_repl: line numbers below count literal LF bytes in the executable; these are intact readable embedded JS, not decompiled native code, and can be reproduced by read_bytes().split(b'\n'). N:56861-56916 sends {type:'trusted_service_request',id,exec_id,service,request}; validates nonempty service and JSON-serializable request, tracks asynchronous operations, returns response.value plus output/content side effects. Trusted loadHandler imports configured service modules, checks export handleRpc and caches handlers (N:58665-58716); worker sends {type:'trusted_service_response',id,output,content_items,ok,value} or {ok:false,error:error.message} (N:58718-58757). Kernel rejects new Error(response.error) (N:56869-56873). Therefore Error.name, code, reason, retryable, stack are LOST across ordinary RPC; only message survives. Browser recovery is the explicit envelope exception and reconstructs a named error on the client (browser-client.js:130-242).
Own wrappers see original Error before loss: /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/services/browser.mjs:157-164 rejectionKind tests BrowserServiceError/code -> service:<code>, BrowserUseSecurityError/reason -> security:<reason>, BrowserCredentialRecoveryError/details.reason -> recovery:<reason>, ZodError -> invalid_command, otherwise failed. This is specifically used to sanitize secret-substitution failures (195-219), not a global error rewriter. Runtime launch registers own sky/browser paths and includes their real directories plus secrets support and own browser-service directory in trusted code roots; /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/runtime/launch.mjs:94-156. The vendor cua-repl launcher respects NODE_REPL_TRUSTED_SERVICES override (launch.js:71) so no vendor source patch is needed.
IMPORTANT exposure distinction: cells DO NOT receive privileged nodeRepl APIs. Kernel builds base bridge with filtered/frozen env (N:55500-55523), base {cwd,env,homeDir,tmpDir,requestMeta,write,emitImage[,emitAudio]} (N:59023-59123), then adds rpc and freezes/locks it (N:56884-56917). write accepts any value via formatLog and optional nonempty itemId. emitImage awaits its argument, normalizes data/file URLs, bytes or {bytes,mimeType}, sends emit_image host request, tracks completion; image values never directly call a codec in @oai/cua (N:58940-58973,59059-59085). createElicitation, authenticated fetch, config, telemetry, nativePipe, hooks, launchServices, setResponseMeta and withSuspendedTimeout are on the separate trusted-worker privileged bridge, NOT the untrusted cell global (N:57725-57952,58645-58657). No separate nodeRepl.elicitation method found; protocol operation is elicitation and method is createElicitation. Privileged config methods: readToml,writeToml,read,readRequirements,writeValue,batchWrite (N:58267-58274). fetch converts Request to method/url/headers/body_base64 and returns Response from status/headers/body_base64 (N:57484-57546); it deliberately does not extend exec drain. Optional telemetry bridge requires NODE_REPL_TRACE_META=1 (N:58640-58650); otel.log is always a best-effort privileged host message (N:57908-57925).
Q2 addendum: vendor browser docs can append CUA_REPL_BROWSER_DOCUMENTATION_ADDENDUM only when browser surface enabled and value nonblank; R/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:66560-66569. This is separate from cua's MCP host rules.

Q4 CLIENT-SIDE LIMITS / BEHAVIOURS (ownership is important)
CORRECTION TO THE PREMISE: the 3 s default/cap is not a timer in @oai/cua's browser client. Client passes timeoutMs through as command.timeout_ms and (if >0) client_timeout_ms (browser-client.js:6812-6828,7720-7731,10024-10034). Vendor browser SERVICE clamps with qe: default 3000, max t.max||3000, min 0 (R/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:18546-18548). Raw CDP also defaults 3000 there (54219-54224). The 30 s default is node_repl CELL timeout, not cua-repl package: installed N:55276 contains 'The default timeout is 30000 ms (30 seconds); increase timeout_ms for longer operations.' The thin cua-repl launcher sets neither timeout. Own browser service already lifts per-phase budgets to 120 s, default 3 s; /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/mcp/surface.mjs:91-102 and own-browser-service-design.md:61-65 describe that intentional difference. Replacing @oai/cua alone does not lift a vendor service's cap.
Actual client limits/defaults beyond those:
- Browser evaluate serializes string as `const arg = <JSON>;\nreturn (<text>);`; a function is stringified and called with await, locator functions receive element/elements plus arg. No lexical closure crosses. Empty/non-string/function rejected; arg must JSON.stringify successfully. browser-client.js:6717-6764. String statement lists are intentionally not accepted by this wrapper.
- Runtime proxy hides disabled APIs as undefined / absent from `in`, ownKeys and descriptors, respects per-browser supported defaults/overrides, wraps results and unwraps API arguments; constructor identity maps to manifest interfaces; browser-client.js:10061-10212. Merely retaining docs without this proxy changes feature-detection semantics.
- Recovery envelope validation includes schema_version=1; command /^[a-z_]{1,64}$/; allowed phase set; enumerated reason/next_action pair and security_reason consistency. Unknown envelope -> 'Browser service returned an invalid recovery response'. browser-client.js:189-242,10272-10284. Orbit-only message-tail replacement at 10285-10308. This is cloud-specific baggage unless vendor mode remains supported.
- Locators validate basic arguments, synthesize selector strings and RegExp escapes, enforce same-tab composition, cache collection reads by relative selector with failed-read eviction and cache clearing after local actions. browser-client.js:6766-6769,6777-7465,7467-7628. Action failures trigger an additional evaluateAll with timeoutMs:1000, max 5 match samples and 120 chars text each; append 'Locator diagnostics: {...}' classified by strict-mode/interception/match/visibility (6685-6715,7216-7249). This observable extra RPC is easy to overlook and should be consciously retained or dropped.
- downloadMedia defaults 120000; waitForEvent('download') defaults 120000; only download/filechooser supported; waitForTimeout requires nonnegative integer and sends a client budget of requested wait+2000; expectNavigation starts waiter before callback and Promise.all joins, returns callback result. browser-client.js:7251-7260,7796-7863. get_visible_dom() has no options, sends only browser_id/tab_id, so exposing new caps requires a client API change (7910-7916).
- createBrowserTab validates browser ID; prefixes https:// for nonparseable URLs; resolves browser and loads its docs; applies nameSession and visibility BEFORE tabs.new; goto(url??about:blank); closes newly created tab if navigation unsupported; then MUST await getAXState({disableDiffing:true,emit:false}) and emit it before returning handle. create_browser_api.js:75-111 and helper 26-36. A successful new tab can therefore exist even if final snapshot rejects/times out. There is no client-side 60 s create timer: own host guidance 'can take 60 s' is outer timeout advice (surface.mjs:110).
- getTab distinguishes id/providerTabId, mention and absolute-URL references; URL references require explicit browser, exact one match, and merged user/managed inventory. Managed tabs used directly, user tabs claimed if supported. Mentions validate vendor plugin URL syntax/fields and exact title+URL, enforce matching profile; stale/ambiguous/not-found errors are explicit. New-tab special cases emit metadata without AX snapshot. create_browser_api.js:113-210,259-289; tab_reference.js:3-58.
- getApp(mac) requires string; getApp(Linux/Windows) requires positive safe integer windowId, obtains state before returning; mac observation passes disableDiffing -> disableDiff. bind_mac_app.js:7-49,148-155; create_tinysky_alt.js:60-141. getState uses allSettled: failing app inventory does not destroy browsers and vice versa; errors array omitted when empty. Per-browser failures preserve metadata with tabs:[] and error sentence. Browser tab inventory merges user tabs then managed by id; openTabs failure console.error then [] rather than rejecting. get_state.js:18-43; get_browsers.js:6-37; get_browser_tabs.js:5-21.
- Emission/defaults: observations and inventories auto-write; emit:false suppresses result but not first-use docs. App screenshot null -> Screenshot unavailable; combined state may omit screenshot. bind_mac_app.js:14-49; core/display.js:3-23. DOM-only Tab maps getAXState to playwright.domSnapshot, getScreenshot to screenshot(); missing observation APIs throw. Native input wrappers remain present but throw 'This tab does not support accessibility input. Use its Playwright API.' Index-based text input requires integer >=0 or null. bind_tab.js:4-101.
- Platform guards: mac scroll rejects object/pixels, paste defaults text. Linux scroll rejects page numbers; paste only text; selectText/setValue throw locally. Windows screenshot emit:false rejected before capture; screenshot ID cached for coordinates, cleared at next observation, multiple screenshot regions rejected by one_screenshot; scroll requires coordinate point and pixels, selectText unavailable, paste only text. bind_mac_app.js:51-57,93-110; bind_linux_app.js:103-137; bind_windows_app.js:9-27,80-161,163-179; one_screenshot.js:1-10; require_plain_text.js:2-5; core/positive_pixels.js:2-6.
- AX diffing is NOT computed by mac facade: it forwards disableDiff to Sky and returns native skyshot.text. get_app_state.js:8-18; targets/mac/client.js:34-47; window_result.js:5-41. Linux JS formats the tree but always full state; Windows composes native accessibility.tree plus selected/focused strings; no facade tree diff. Screenshot bytes decoder only base64/file-read, does NOT downscale: core/screenshot_bytes.js:3-11. No evidence of a universal @oai/cua or node_repl image downscale found; scaling is native/browser screenshot backend or consuming host (see Q6). Do not recreate a guessed downscale in the facade.
- 'Apps ask once per connection' is not a facade permission Set. Mac POLICY wrapper runs in trusted Sky service, queries native getAppPolicy, denies forbidden/org-denied before input, uses privileged createElicitation with connector_id='computer-use', app bundle ID, persist choices session/(maybe always); accepts only action:'accept'; otherwise 'Computer Use was not approved to use <displayName>'. R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/project/cua/sky_js/src/targets/mac/computer-use-policy.js:12-137. It recognizes persisted-state response to avoid duplicate approval telemetry (94-101). Own MCP persists accepted responses with _meta.persist (surface.mjs:355-361); its connection owns random session ID, deletes corresponding CODEX_HOME/computer-use/sessions/<session>.toml on close (connection.mjs:68-81). Approval scope survives idle runtime stop because connection persists (connection.mjs:12-19). Copying this policy into untrusted facade would weaken/misplace the boundary.
- Cancellation/timeouts: browser client itself has no AbortController/setTimeout cancellation engine. node_repl tracks async host operations; Sky policy suspends outer timeout around native action after approval (computer-use-policy.js:16-17,137; embedded N:57776-57800). cua MCP cancellation can withdraw queued work, but forwards cancellation for in-flight calls without promising it stops JS; task.mjs:65-84, server.mjs:329-335; model rule surface.mjs:174 says only timeout_ms stops running cell. Cell timeout resets kernel/loses handles per surface.mjs:91-94 (existing host finding; not re-probed in this read-only pass). Core docs' no-sleep/observe-act-verify workflow is guidance, not a client-enforced scheduler.

Q5 DEPENDENCIES / NETWORK / LICENSING
Distinguish shipped bytes from imports on the actual tinyskyAlt -> trusted RPC path. @oai/cua and @oai/cua-repl package.json both say dependencies:{} (R/cua_node/@oai/cua/package.json:23; cua-repl/package.json:24), because dependencies are bundled. The model-side browser-client.js is self-contained, contains Zod (Zod exports at 530 onward; schemas throughout) plus API classes/serializers; it has no fetch/network/telemetry call in its executed setup path beyond nodeRepl.rpc (10215-10311) and emitImage. The REPL-side facade uses Node builtins (filesystem, URL, etc.) and vendored tslib (dist/lib/js/oai_js/node_modules/tslib/tslib.es6.js:1 onward). It does NOT need markdown-it, image decoder, Statsig or LevelDB to implement the surface. browser-client.mjs is a separate bundled variant also exporting setupBrowserRuntime (10642 onward); tinyskyAlt explicitly imports browser-client.js, not .mjs (create_tinysky_alt.js:148-149).
Physical packages: Statsig @statsig/js-client and @statsig/client-core 3.32.6 are vendored as separate dist/node_modules/.pnpm trees (all files individually inventoried in Q1); client-core/src/StatsigMetadata.js:1 onward provides version metadata, NetworkConfig.js:17-20 default public endpoints. The much larger bundled browser-service.mjs ALSO embeds its own Statsig 3.33.1 (4605-4608), Zod (21419-21420), markdown-it (20038-20064) with URL/linkify/Unicode helper code (16244,16818-16885; punycode reports 2.3.1 at 4067-4075), zxing-wasm 3.1.2 barcode reader (32169;34900-34920) and Sentry node/instrumentation SDK code (61592 onward,66338,69611). Do not mistake these backend dependencies for required REPL dependencies. There is a local browser-accessibility.wasm.br and zxing_reader.wasm in installed package; readable tree omits those binaries. Accessibility loads with Node WASI, fs and zlib.brotliDecompressSync (10500-10525), not an npm image codec. No sharp/jpeg-js/pngjs package or invocation was found in these two package trees; browser images are obtained via the browser/native service and emitted as bytes. Exact complete SBOM/version/license provenance for code flattened into browser-service.mjs is NOT recoverable from package.json alone; no sourcemap or bundled-license comment was present in these three shipping script files. The detected embedded libraries above are positive identifications, not a claim of a complete transitive SBOM.
The nested skill/node_modules has nine npm package manifests, all declaring MIT: classic-level 3.0.0, abstract-level 3.1.1, level-supports 6.2.0, level-transcoder 1.0.1, module-error 1.0.2, maybe-combine-errors 1.0.0, node-gyp-build 4.8.4, napi-macros 2.2.2, is-buffer 2.0.5. Each citation is R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/node_modules/<package>/package.json:1-8. Their native tree also ships LevelDB 1.20 and Snappy sources/build files and classic-level.node prebuild. Installed corresponding classic-level/deps/leveldb/leveldb-1.20/LICENSE:1-27 and deps/snappy/snappy/COPYING:1-26 are BSD-3-clause-style notices requiring source notice retention and binary notice reproduction. MIT LICENSE files omitted from readable extraction are present in installed package (Q1 lists each). These are for browser backend profile/storage work, not a locator facade.
Network/account baggage exists in shipped but mostly non-cell code:
- bundled targets/mac/computer-use-telemetry.js:6-9,122-188 logs Statsig events through privileged nodeRepl.fetch to ab.chatgpt.com/v1 and chatgpt.com/ces/v1/rgstr, fetches chatgpt.com/backend-api/me and associates user id/email; turn/thread/item/model/reasoning metadata at 44-120. Disabled if NODE_REPL_DISABLE_ANALYTICS=1 or BROWSER_USE_DISABLE_AMBIENT_NETWORK=1 (220-223) and inactive without privileged config/fetch (225-230). In RPC mode this policy/telemetry is in actual @oai/sky service, not the cell; replacing @oai/cua alone does NOT remove the service's copy.
- embedded browser-service.mjs uses authenticated host fetch for Statsig configuration/event policy (10240-10395), chatgpt.com/backend-api/aura/identity (10404-10416), OpenAI Statsig CDN/registry endpoints (4559-4561), and Sentry export with a shipped DSN (70175-70205). A zxing CDN fallback string is present (32169), but normal initialization supplies local wasm bytes (34900-34920); do not claim that CDN is contacted in ordinary operation. Cloud environment includes handoff URL chatgpt.com/browser/handoff (58511) and /home/oai/.config/gaas-browser/config.json constant (70220); no such files were read.
- own launch explicitly sets NODE_REPL_DISABLE_ANALYTICS:'1' (/Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/runtime/launch.mjs:136-139). That is evidence of configuration, not a live network trace or proof every third-party service suppresses every request.
Licensing recommendation: implement the public facade independently and depend on published open-source packages only if genuinely needed. Do NOT assume permission to redistribute OpenAI source/docs/WASM because package private:false or dependencies:{}; neither @oai/cua nor @oai/cua-repl has a package license field or own LICENSE in these inventories. The runtime-level cua_node/LICENSE is Node.js and third-party notices, not a license grant for all OpenAI code (installed runtime cua_node/LICENSE:1-50). Internal reading/quotation is authorized by this task; shipping verbatim vendor docs or source needs a separate provenance/licensing decision. No endpoint/account files or secrets were accessed.

Q6 AX TEXT / INDEXING / DOM / SCREENSHOTS
macOS native app: the numbered AX tree, focus/selection text and AX diff come from Sky native, NOT tinyskyAlt JS. Facade -> get_app_state -> native client ComputerUseIPCAppGetSkyshotRequest -> result.skyshot.text; JS only validates string and optionally prepends app_specific_instructions once per app (except Numbers). Citations R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/project/cua/sky_js/src/targets/mac/client.js:8,34-47; get_app_state.js:8-18; window_result.js:5-79; tinysky_alt/bind_mac_app.js:7-11. Additional native binary corroboration, same LF-count citation convention as Q3: installed @oai/sky/Codex Computer Use.app/Contents/MacOS/SkyComputerUseService:74055 (byte 17955457) contains 'The focused UI element is ', followed by 'Selected text: ```'; line 73984 contains AXTreeDiffingEnabled/lastAXTree. Exact literal 'Selected:' was not located in this executable; that does not change boundary evidence (whole text is native output), but do not claim that exact spelling was independently matched. No native implementation source was available here to specify its full format algorithm.
Browser AX-capable tabs are a THIRD path: service-side browser-accessibility WASM (not mac Sky and not REPL facade) builds revisions from CDP nodes and assigns/looks up element identities; service JS controls full vs auto/diff mode and freshness. Bundled service R/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/scripts/browser-service.mjs:45327-45415,45865-45923; buildRevision(previous,nodes,{mode:disableDiffing?'full':'auto'}) and rendered=x.text at 45911-45916. WASM revision_text_length/copy_text and identity_length/copy_identity exports are wrapped at 10759-10832. Actual trusted @oai/browser-desktop service has the corresponding WASM implementation; copying @oai/cua's JS API does not replace this backend dependency.
Linux is an exception to 'all AX formatting is native': lower-level sky_js assigns numeric IDs with a per-window native-id map (targets/linux/element_ids.js:2-34); full tree formatter in targets/linux/accessibility_tree.js:17-174 filters/collapses nodes, prints tab-indented '<id> <role> (<states>) <properties>', escapes CR/LF/TAB, labels Secondary Actions, and merges redundant text/container nodes. sky.js:104-111 attaches this formatter after RPC; bind_linux_app.js:20-27 prefixes Accessibility source. Numeric ID assignment belongs to trusted service's lower-level client on the RPC path; tree-to-string is attached in untrusted facade. Windows binder composes provided accessibility.tree with 'Focused element: ...' and 'Selected text: ...' (bind_windows_app.js:199-212), rather than the mac wording.
DOM-only tabs: bind_tab.js:65-78 calls tab.playwright.domSnapshot()/tab.screenshot(). browser-client.js:7890-7898 merely unwraps `.dom_snapshot`; it does not build DOM text or assign AX numbers. Service executes injected Playwright incrementalAriaSnapshot({mode:'ai'}) on body/documentElement, expands visible iframe bodies where allowed, then normalizes rendered snapshot; bundled browser-service.mjs:42056-42144,42218 onward. The rendered DOM snapshot strips [ref=...] and [cursor=...] annotations, unwraps unlabeled generic/listitem/group containers and drops unlabeled img nodes (browser-service.mjs:42257-42290), so it does not expose the numbered mac AX element index contract; the native input wrappers correctly refuse DOM-only tabs (bind_tab.js:81-92). DOM-cua visible-DOM node IDs are another separate API, and its output/options are service-owned (browser-client.js:7910-7916).
Screenshot pipeline:
1) mac native service produces skyshot.screenshot.url, JS decodes a data URL or reads file URL into Uint8Array (window_result.js:10-30; core/screenshot_bytes.js:3-11), optional nodeRepl.emitImage (core/display.js:14-23). No JS resizing in this path.
2) DOM browser screenshot is captured by trusted browser service via CDP, JPEG quality 80; CSS scale computes 1/devicePixelRatio (device mode 1), clip/fullPage are explicit, normal capture prefers screencast with 2 s wait then capture fallback with 5 s budget (device-mode fallback 2 s). Bundled browser-service.mjs:36620-36745; browser-client.js:9467-9492 maps clip into cropX/cropY/cropWidth/cropHeight and decodes base64. This is backend pixel-scale selection, not a post-REPL image downscale.
3) emitImage normalizes bytes and ships emit_image host message; embedded N:58940-58973,59059-59085 contains no resize. Own MCP correctImages sniffs image MIME because node_repl can label JPEG as PNG, without changing bytes (/Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/mcp/surface.mjs:264-274).
The host instruction 'apply the host’s downscale' is not evidence of an @oai/cua downscale implementation. Exact downstream host image resizing or native Sky scale algorithm remains outside this verified source pass. Keep original returned byte coordinates and consumer display coordinates distinct; do not invent a universal numeric factor.

Q7 COMPATIBILITY BOUNDARY / WHAT MUST MATCH / WHAT IS FREE
Recommendation: replace the small facade and launcher, keep the node_repl engine and trusted service contracts unchanged initially. Treat compatibility as the observable JS/object/output contract of the surfaces cua actually enables, NOT copying every dormant vendor backend/cloud feature. @oai/cua's shipped mass includes backends, native transports, generated declarations, tests and telemetry that need not enter the replacement.
Must retain for drop-in behavior:
(1) Bootstrap sets persistent global cua, initializes only enabled providers, emits core before first cell result; initialize aliases getState; rewriteDocumentation and global agent for browser APIs. Required/unknown surface checks and intentional absence of disabled surfaces. R/.../tinysky_alt/globals.js:3-27,create_tinysky_alt.js:16-55.
(2) Facade signatures/data shapes below, including positional app-vs-tab input difference; Uint8Array screenshots; missing screenshot vs thrown screenshot-only error; Promise<void> actions; optional errors and undefined/absent properties rather than substitute null. Discovery/binding must keep identity/profile resolution, exact URL matches, claim-vs-managed rules, and initial full snapshot side effects (Q4).
(3) Browser object's whole manifest-advertised surface, NOT only the short initial core's Browser interface. The checked-in api.json has 22 interfaces, 147 members, 66 named types (member count verified below by extraction); each browser filters this by setup disabledMemberIds, type/default support and apiSupportOverrides. Capabilities are separately discovered and documented (management,visibility,viewport; tab cdp,botDetection,browserAuth,pageAssets,webmcp), not necessarily enabled on cua. All runtime methods that the selected service exposes need faithful wrapper/serialization, including locator chaining/regex escaping, event join ordering, return unwrapping and output. A manifest alone cannot generate all behavior: e.g. evaluate serialization and locator object ownership need code.
(4) RPC payload field spelling/casing and shapes must stay exact while own service wrappers remain: sky {type,method,args}; browser {method,params:{type,browser_id,tab_id,...}}; secret substitutions are exact-whole-value and pinned shapes. /Users/new/Developer/GitHub/cua/src/services/sky.mjs:6-26,41-55; browser.mjs:14-23,195-219. Introducing extra keys into eligible secret-input requests can fail closed. Do not expose privileged nodeRepl APIs to cells.
(5) Plain Error sentences are a real contract: RPC loses other Error fields; model behavior and existing parity corpus use sentences. Preserve vendor sentences for kept operations, plus caller-side message appendages such as locator diagnostics and invalid-recovery response (Q3/Q4). Do not promise to preserve thrown class names except locally constructed client errors; service-specific names cannot survive current engine. Full first-party throw-string index follows.
(6) Output ordering/duplication matters: core once, browser docs once per browser, then state; emit:false result suppression but docs still present; write content IDs cua.core/cua.browser.<id>/cua.state; rewriteDocumentation same-requestMeta suppression; screenshot MIME must remain correct downstream. Documentation is part of the model contract, but literal vendor prose is not technically required for API compatibility. A byte-for-byte goal would require shipping the pinned static files and preserving generated docs ordering, gates, browser identity/capability strings and host addenda; this is a product/licensing decision, not an implicit requirement of JS compatibility.
Freedom when preserving that boundary: internal module names/files, class names, dependency choice (including replace Zod with explicit validation where needed), launch process organization, caching implementation that leaves observable semantics intact, telemetry removal, vendor cloud/auth/backend code removal when never advertised, own documentation wording/rules with intentional review. Do not reimplement @oai/sky native AX rendering or browser DOM/screenshot service as part of this package swap. Lifting limits, adding visible-DOM options, dropping diagnostics, changing auto-snapshot behavior or changing string evaluate semantics is an intentional behavior revision and must be documented/tested; it is not invisible plumbing freedom.
Legacy exports: package '.' exports a different legacy cua with initialize/computer/browsers/documentation (dist/lib/js/oai_js_cua/src/cua.js:6-25); ./tinyskyBrowser exposes injectable setup/read_documentation factory (tinysky_browser/create_tinysky_browser.js:8-55). Current cua-repl imports only ./tinyskyAlt. Reproduce legacy entrypoints only if external consumers are within #116's scope, not automatically. nodeRepl.write/emitImage belong to retained engine, and createElicitation/fetch/config belong to trusted engine bridge; do not rebuild them just because they appear in package docs.
Additional Q4 detail discovered while checking exact validation: Command constructor stores schema but toJSON DOES NOT parse (browser-client.js:495-509); ordinary transport.send only calls toJSON (10024-10029). Therefore schema definitions sitting in the client are NOT proof that every payload constraint is client-enforced. Explicit client parse sites are tabs.content (6569), DOM node IDs (7997), botDetection request/result (8617/8625), browserAuth request/result (8975/8979), user tab-context result (6627), CDP results (8724/8739), assets results (9113/9123), WebMCP fetched tool results (9176), management results (9697/9708), visibility/viewport results (9785/9793/9857/9865), side-effect envelope (10038). BrowserAuth's explicitly parsed local limits are max 6 fields/field_ids, 2-10 options, option label max120, otpHandle max16384, safe identifier exclusions; schema 8780-8822 and request parse 8919-8979. Side-effect display truncation is 100000 chars (10258-10260; renderer 246-345), not a universal limit on direct nodeRepl.write or facade AX text. WebMCP fetchTools returns frozen description()/call() object bound to exact registration IDs; unknown names must refetch (9176-9233). README says empty surfaces fail but actual globals/launcher permit an empty Set; implementation takes precedence (README:19-21 vs globals.js:8-23/launch.js:20-31). Type comment 'focus within 250ms' is stale relative to actual service 3000ms, so use implementation not declaration comment (types.d.ts:92-97 vs @oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:51538-51544).

Companion .d.ts files are declaration-only mirrors; tinysky_alt/test/*.d.ts are declarations for tests/mock providers, not code required by public runtime. oai_js_core and oai_js_types are utility/type-only support; oai_js/tslib is compiler helper code. project/cua/sky_js includes lower-level native transport/platform clients, which are largely dormant in untrusted cells when sky RPC is configured; retain only the RPC facade plus Linux tree formatting when replacing @oai/cua, not a second native service. cua-repl/bin/cua-repl.mjs is entrypoint, dist/.../launch.js is env/spawn/signal/exit wrapper, dist/.../instructions.js is file selection/assembly, instructions/*.md/js are model-facing tool/banner docs, plugin/ is Codex host registration and can be omitted from a cua-only replacement if not used.

NEXT DECISION FOR #116
Choose the compatibility target explicitly: (a) cua's enabled computer + own browser-service API, preserving current model calls and trusted wrappers (recommended), or (b) full vendor package replacement including dormant cloud/auth/legacy exports. Then build static contract fixtures from the signatures/error index and a fake nodeRepl.rpc transcript harness before implementation; no live browser is required to verify dispatch, documentation ordering, hidden-member behavior, return conversion and error rewrapping. Separately decide whether docs are independently authored semantic equivalents or exact vendor text; independent implementation and model API compatibility do not themselves grant redistribution rights. Remaining evidence gaps: complete flattened-bundle SBOM/licenses and exact downstream/native image-resize algorithm.
